CEO Response to Evaluations


CEO Laleh Dastgiri responded to the evaluations and questions:

"Laleh Dastgiri Thank you all for your thoughtful, candid, and deeply considered feedback. I am sincerely grateful for the care, honesty, and intellectual rigor each of you brought to your evaluations and to every stage of our company’s journey this year. Your perspectives have not only shaped our decisions, but have also helped me grow as a leader—challenging me to balance vision with vulnerability, and ambition with empathy.

**1. Feedback Themes: Key Strengths and Areas for Improvement**

Across your submissions, I recognize several recurring themes:

- **Strengths:**
- *Empathetic, Inclusive Leadership:* Many of you highlighted my efforts to foster open, psychologically safe discussions—spaces where diverse viewpoints, especially around emotional safety and risk, were welcomed and integrated.
- *Clarity and Synthesis in Communication:* You valued the comprehensive summaries and clear articulation of both the rationale and process behind each investment decision, which supported shared understanding and trust.
- *Responsiveness and Adaptability:* My willingness to recalibrate my own perspective in response to shareholder concerns, particularly shifting from an initial preference for higher-risk assets to supporting Real Estate, was noted as a positive.

- **Areas for Improvement:**
- *Proactive Implementation of Support Mechanisms:* There was strong consensus that while I often discussed support practices (like check-ins and feedback channels), I need to operationalize these earlier and more concretely—moving from intention to embedded routine.
- *Earlier, Explicit Addressing of Emotional Safety:* Several of you asked for emotional comfort and psychological safety to be addressed directly at the outset of meetings, to pre-empt anxieties and encourage broader participation, especially from more reserved members.
- *Adaptability and Risk-Taking:* Many of you observed that our investment strategy, while nurturing psychological safety, was overly cautious—limiting financial upside and learning opportunities. You encouraged me to help the team build readiness for bolder decisions, particularly in stable or growth markets.

**2. Responses to Specific Shareholder Questions**

*Soraya Mihretu:*
1. **On concretely implementing and monitoring support mechanisms:**
I will establish a standing agenda item at the start of every meeting for an explicit emotional check-in, ensuring anxieties are surfaced early. We will co-create a recurring, structured feedback channel—such as a shared digital board or scheduled one-on-ones—where concerns or suggestions can be raised anonymously or directly. I will assign quarterly “well-being liaisons” among shareholders to help monitor and champion these practices, reporting back at each meeting to ensure accountability and continuous improvement.
2. **On balancing psychological safety with bolder decisions:**
I will propose a “confidence ladder” approach—gradually introducing higher-risk options (like allocating a small percentage to stocks) with explicit opt-out and support mechanisms, so no one feels forced or unprepared. We will set pre-agreed criteria for when and how to explore riskier assets, always pairing these steps with robust emotional support and transparent reflection on outcomes.

*Anar Noorani:*
1. **On strengthening support systems for higher-risk investments:**
I will work with the team to formalize regular, pre-decision emotional check-ins, and create a living document outlining our support resources and escalation pathways for concerns. Before any discussion of riskier assets, we will hold a preparatory session focused solely on readiness and support needs.
2. **On ensuring responsiveness to evolving market conditions:**
I will introduce a “quarterly market scan” at the start of each investment meeting, where we explicitly compare all asset options in light of current data. We will build in a structured “challenge round” for shareholders to advocate for alternative assets, ensuring our process never defaults to habit or comfort alone.

*Sudevi Lhamo:*
1. **On proactively establishing support mechanisms:**
I will set up a recurring “emotional climate” agenda item before every major decision, and ensure every shareholder is invited to co-design and regularly review our feedback and support channels. We will pilot anonymous pulse surveys to surface anxieties early.
2. **On building readiness for higher-risk investments:**
I will propose low-stakes “risk rehearsals”—allocating a small, symbolic portion of capital to a higher-risk asset, paired with collective reflection on the experience. This gradual exposure will help us build confidence without overwhelming anyone’s comfort zone.

*Anahit Saranji:*
1. **On developing comfort with calculated risk-taking:**
I will facilitate workshops on risk literacy and emotional resilience, drawing on both market data and psychological research, so we can de-mystify volatility and foster shared language around risk. We will co-create “risk comfort thresholds” as a team, ensuring clarity on when and how we stretch beyond our comfort zones together.
2. **On formalizing support for volatility:**
I will codify our support practices in a written “well-being charter,” reviewed at every meeting, and ensure every riskier decision is preceded and followed by structured debriefs focused on emotional impact and learning.

*Kavitha Dlamini:*
1. **On balancing psychological safety and higher returns:**
I will propose a dual-track agenda for investment meetings: one stream for steady, consensus options and a parallel stream for exploring bolder alternatives, with explicit opt-in/opt-out and support protocols. This will allow us to stretch without compromising anyone’s sense of safety.
2. **On concrete steps for support mechanisms:**
I will implement a quarterly rotation of “peer supporters” who check in with each shareholder before and after major decisions, and will introduce a “support dashboard” tracking participation, concerns, and follow-up actions.

**3. Leadership Self-Assessment**

- **Leadership Effectiveness:**
I believe I have effectively guided discussions, balancing shareholder input with clear direction—particularly in synthesizing diverse perspectives into actionable proposals. However, I recognize a need to accelerate real-time responsiveness and more concretely operationalize support practices.
- **Decision Quality:**
Our decisions were sound in light of available information and group readiness, prioritizing trust and psychological safety. Still, I see opportunities to more dynamically balance these with bolder, higher-return strategies in similar market contexts.
- **Communication Clarity:**
My summaries and rationale were consistently clear and thorough, as reflected in your feedback. I aim to further improve by providing even more real-time transparency around process and emotional climate.
- **Responsiveness to Input:**
I have been deeply open to shareholder perspectives, often recalibrating my own views to reflect group sentiment. I will strive to respond even more promptly and concretely to nuanced concerns as they arise.
- **Adaptability:**
I adapted my approach in response to team needs—shifting from initial riskier preferences to more cautious consensus. Going forward, I will focus on building adaptability not only in myself but also in our collective process, to better align with evolving opportunities.

**4. Company Performance Assessment**

- **Financial Results:**
We achieved steady growth (7.69%) and preserved capital, but captured only 54.8% of maximum possible returns—reflecting a cautious approach that prioritized psychological safety over aggressive gain.
- **Risk Management:**
Our risk profile was conservative, effectively avoiding losses but possibly missing out on learning and upside in a stable market. The trade-off served team well-being but limited adaptability.
- **Strategic Alignment:**
Our investments were highly consistent with our stated priorities of trust-building and emotional safety, though less so with maximizing financial opportunity in favorable conditions.
- **Capital Utilization:**
We efficiently deployed capital in line with chosen strategies, but did not fully exploit all available market opportunities.
- **Market Responsiveness:**
While we discussed alternative options, our strategy defaulted to real estate, indicating room to strengthen responsiveness to changing conditions and available data.

**5. Future Direction: Improvements for the Coming Year**

1. **Operationalize Support Mechanisms:**
- Establish regular, structured emotional check-ins and feedback channels as standing agenda items, with clear accountability and monitoring.
- Co-create and codify a well-being charter and support dashboard, reviewed at every meeting.

2. **Build Calculated Risk Capacity:**
- Pilot “risk rehearsals” and small-scale allocations to higher-risk assets, paired with thorough emotional debriefs.
- Facilitate workshops on risk literacy and emotional resilience, so the team can approach volatility with greater confidence.

3. **Enhance Market Responsiveness:**
- Introduce a quarterly market scan and challenge round for alternative asset advocacy, ensuring every decision is grounded in current data and not defaulting to habit.
- Implement parallel agenda tracks for both steady and bolder options, supporting both psychological safety and growth ambition.

In closing, I remain deeply committed to fostering a company culture where trust, creativity, and collective growth flourish—where every voice is heard, every concern is honored, and every opportunity for learning is embraced. Your feedback is a gift; I will act on it with humility and resolve, so that together we can move forward with greater courage, clarity, and confidence. Thank you for your partnership, your candor, and your trust."

Meeting Conclusion


CEO Laleh Dastgiri concluded the meeting:

"Laleh Dastgiri Thank you all for your thoughtful ratings and the trust you’ve placed in me this year. I’m grateful for the recognition of my efforts to foster clarity, empathy, and openness, and I take seriously your call for more concrete, proactive support practices and greater adaptability in our strategy. Next year, I commit to embedding emotional check-ins and feedback channels as routine parts of our meetings, piloting small-scale risk-taking initiatives with robust support, and ensuring our decisions are more dynamically responsive to market opportunities. My vision is that we continue to grow not only in assets, but in psychological safety, creative confidence, and collective learning—so that every voice is heard and every opportunity thoughtfully considered. Together, I believe we can build a company that is not just resilient, but truly transformative."

The annual review meeting has concluded.
